⏺ Update(~/git/cm3/m3-sys/m3front/src/msir/MSIRBuilder.m3)
  ⎿  Added 45 lines
      417      RETURN result;
      418    END EmitCall;
      419  
      420 +PROCEDURE EmitMethodCall(name: TEXT;  obj: MSIR.Value;  midx: 
          +LONGINT;                                                      
      421 +                          rtype: MSIR.T;                      
      422 +                          READONLY args: ARRAY OF MSIR.Value):
          + MSIR.Value =                                                 
      423 +  VAR                                                         
      424 +    ptrT    := MSIR.TPtr(MSIR.TVoid());                       
      425 +    suite   : MSIR.Value;                                     
      426 +    slotPtr : MSIR.Value;                                     
      427 +    fn      : MSIR.Value;                                     
      428 +    allArgs : REF ARRAY OF MSIR.Value;                        
      429 +    nArgs   := NUMBER(args);                                  
      430 +    unwind  : MSIR.Block;                                     
      431 +    normalB : MSIR.Block;                                     
      432 +    result  : MSIR.Value;                                     
      433 +  BEGIN                                                       
      434 +    (* 1. Load vtable pointer (first word of object). *)      
      435 +    suite := MSIR.BuildLoad(curBlock, "", ptrT, obj);         
      436 +                                                              
      437 +    (* 2. Advance to the method slot (idx * sizeof(ptr) bytes)
          +. *)                                                          
      438 +    IF midx = 0L THEN                                         
      439 +      slotPtr := suite;                                       
      440 +    ELSE                                                      
      441 +      slotPtr := MSIR.BuildPtrAdd(curBlock, "", suite, midx); 
      442 +    END;                                                      
      443 +                                                              
      444 +    (* 3. Load function pointer from the slot. *)             
      445 +    fn := MSIR.BuildLoad(curBlock, "", ptrT, slotPtr);        
      446 +                                                              
      447 +    (* 4. Build argument list: obj (implicit self) first, then
          + explicit args. *)                                            
      448 +    allArgs := NEW(REF ARRAY OF MSIR.Value, 1 + nArgs);       
      449 +    allArgs[0] := obj;                                        
      450 +    FOR k := 0 TO nArgs - 1 DO allArgs[1 + k] := args[k] END; 
      451 +                                                              
      452 +    (* 5. Indirect call or invoke depending on TRY context. *)
      453 +    unwind := CurrentUnwindBlock();                           
      454 +    IF unwind # NIL THEN                                      
      455 +      normalB := NewBlock("dispatch.cont");                   
      456 +      result  := MSIR.BuildInvokeIndirect(curBlock, name, fn, 
          +rtype, allArgs^,                                              
      457 +                                            normalB, unwind); 
      458 +      curBlock := normalB;                                    
      459 +    ELSE                                                      
      460 +      result := MSIR.BuildCallIndirect(curBlock, name, fn, rty
          +pe, allArgs^);                                                
      461 +    END;                                                      
      462 +    RETURN result;                                            
      463 +  END EmitMethodCall;                                         
      464 +                                                              
      465  PROCEDURE RegisterProc(v: Value.T;  p: MSIR.Proc) =
      466    BEGIN
      467      IF v = NIL OR p = NIL THEN RETURN END;

⏺ The dispatch IR is exactly right:
  - %t1 = load ptr, ptr %s — load the vtable pointer (first word of object)
  - %t2 = load ptr, ptr %t1 — load the function pointer at slot 0 (area is at
  index 0)
  - %t3 = call i64 %t2(ptr %s) — indirect call with self as first arg

⏺ The generated IR for ShapeDispatch is exactly what the C++ backend produces:

  define i64 @Main__ShapeDispatch(ptr %s) {
  entry:
    %t1 = load ptr, ptr %s      ; vtable pointer = first word of object
    %t2 = load ptr, ptr %t1     ; function ptr at slot 0 (area's index)
    %t3 = call i64 %t2(ptr %s)  ; virtual call with self as first arg
    ret i64 %t3
  }

  What was implemented:

  - Op.PtrAdd — getelementptr ptr, ptr %base, i64 N for vtable slot indexing
  beyond index 0
  - Op.CallIndirect / Op.InvokeIndirect — indirect calls through function
  pointer values (enabling both static and EH-aware dispatch)
  - MSIRBuilder.EmitMethodCall — wires the 3-load sequence + indirect call,
  correctly uses invoke inside TRY contexts
  - QualifyExpr.LhsExpr — exposes the receiver expression for MSIR compilation
  - CallExpr.CompileMSIR — detects QualifyExpr + Value.Class.Method, extracts
  vtable index from Method.Info.offset / Target.Address.size, routes to
  EmitMethodCall
  - Verifier fix — GcRef/GcSlot are valid load/store addresses (heap object
  references)

  What this unlocks: any code that calls methods on OBJECT types can now compile
   through MSIR — including Text, Wr.T, Thread.Mutex, and most standard library
  interfaces that use the object model.

  ---
  Three realistic next steps:

  Heap object field access — now that method dispatch works, the next wall the
  smoke test hits is QualifyExpr.CompileMSIR abandoning for objField class.
  Accessing self.side in SquareArea would require this. The fix: handle
  Class.objField in QualifyExpr.CompileMSIR by computing the field address using
   the object's header offset + field bit-offset. This requires knowing the
  field layout, which Field.Split provides as a bit-offset. One subtlety: the
  CM3 object data starts at byte 8 (after the 8-byte vtable pointer), so field
  offset = 8 + bit_offset/8.

  RAISE + exception descriptors (B) — emitting a minimal static { defn=null, uid
   } struct for each EXCEPTION declaration takes ~30 lines in MSIREmit, then
  RAISE lowers to RTHooks__Raise(act) where act is a stack-allocated
  RaiseActivation. Together these complete the EH story for real programs.

  LOCK (F) — smallest item, maybe 20 lines in LockStmt.CompileMSIR: acquire +
  TRY body FINALLY release. Straightforward given TRY/FINALLY works.

  ---
  Heap object field access is the most immediately impactful — it's the last
  barrier between MSIR and being able to compile most OO M3 code, since field
  reads/writes are far more common than RAISE. But RAISE + value binding
  finishes what we started with EH.

  Which direction?

   the bit offset of the fields region (obj_offset), and Field.Info.offset gives
   the field's bit offset within that region. Total byte offset = (obj_offset + 
  fieldInfo.offset) / 8.

  I'll generalize PtrAdd to byte granularity (one change covers vtable dispatch
  and field access), then handle objField in both CompileMSIR and LValueMSIR.

⏺ 67/67 pass. Here's what landed:

  PtrAdd → byte-granularity GEP — changed from gep ptr (pointer-slot steps) to
  gep i8 (byte steps). The vtable dispatch updated to pass midx * 8 bytes; field
   access passes the raw byte offset. One op covers both uses.

  Heap object field access — QualifyExpr now handles Class.objField in both
  CompileMSIR (field read) and LValueMSIR (field address for writes). The byte
  offset is (fieldOffset_region + fieldInfo.offset) / 8 where fieldOffset_region
   comes from ObjectType.GetFieldsOffsetAndAlign (typically 64 bits = 8 bytes
  for the vtable word).

  What this unlocks: Object field reads and writes compile correctly — including
   self.x, self.y patterns that are ubiquitous in OO M3 code. Combined with
  method dispatch from the previous commit, the full OO model now works:
  obj.method() and obj.field both generate correct LLVM IR.

  Remaining work:
  - RAISE — needs minimal exception descriptor statics, then 3-load sequence +
  RTHooks__Raise
  - Exception value binding (EXCEPT E(v) =>) — extract arg from activation
  record
  - LOCK — TRY/FINALLY wrapper around Thread acquire/release
  - Nested procedures — hardest, up-level access
  - Runtime linking — imports chain, type_cells, GC root maps

❯ What's next?                                                                  

⏺ After this session the MSIR front covers arithmetic, control flow, arrays,
  records, enums, globals, EH (TRY/EXCEPT, TRY/FINALLY), GC barriers, RTLinker
  binder, TYPECASE, method dispatch, and heap object field access.

  Remaining (updated):

  Item: RAISE
  Effort: small–medium
  Blocked by: needs exception descriptor statics
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Item: Exception value binding
  Effort: small
  Blocked by: RAISE
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Item: LOCK
  Effort: small
  Blocked by: nothing — desugars to TRY/FINALLY
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Item: NEW
  Effort: medium
  Blocked by: type_cells for object descriptor
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Item: Runtime linking (type_cells, imports)
  Effort: medium
  Blocked by: —
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Item: Nested procs / up-level
  Effort: large
  Blocked by: —

  ---
  Three realistic next directions:

  RAISE + value binding — finishes the EH story that spans many sessions. RAISE
  needs one static ExceptionDescriptor global per EXCEPTION declaration (just { 
  ptr null, i64 uid }) and a call to RTHooks__Raise with a stack-allocated
  RaiseActivation. Value binding (EXCEPT E(v) =>) then needs ~20 more lines
  extracting the arg from the activation. Together maybe 100 lines.

  LOCK — trivial: LockStmt.CompileMSIR calls Thread.Acquire(mu), wraps the body
  in a TRY/FINALLY, calls Thread.Release(mu) in the finally block. Maybe 25
  lines. Unlocks all thread-safe M3 code.

  NEW + runtime linking step — to create real heap objects, NEW(Dog) must call
  RTHooks__AllocateTracedObj(typeDescr, size) where typeDescr is a pointer to
  the type's RT0.ObjectTypecell. That descriptor must be registered in
  type_cells. This is the first step of true runtime integration — once done,
  the standalone smoke test can actually create and use objects without C
  tricks.

  ---
  My recommendation: LOCK first (it's ~25 lines, unblocked, and completes a
  common programming pattern), then RAISE + value binding to close EH.
  NEW+type_cells is the bigger runtime-linking investment — probably a session
  on its own.

  What direction do you prefer?

          +ytes on 64-bit) *)

      205 +    mu:        MSIR.Value;                                    
      206 +    lpad:      MSIR.Block;                                    
      207 +    finBody:   MSIR.Block;                                    
      208 +    resumeBlk: MSIR.Block;                                    
      209 +    merge:     MSIR.Block;                                    
      210 +    lpSlot:    MSIR.Value;                                    
      211 +    excFlag:   MSIR.Value;                                    
      212 +    lpVal:     MSIR.Value;                                    
      213 +    excFlagV:  MSIR.Value;                                    
      214 +    lpLoaded:  MSIR.Value;                                    
      215 +    lpType:    MSIR.T;                                        
      216 +    zero, one: MSIR.Value;                                    
      217 +  BEGIN                                                       
      218 +    IF NOT MSIRBuilder.InProc() THEN RETURN END;              
      219 +                                                              
      220 +    (* Compile the mutex expression. *)                       
      221 +    mu := Expr.CompileMSIR(p.mutex);                          
      222 +    IF mu = NIL THEN RETURN END;                              
      223 +                                                              
      224 +    (* Acquire the mutex: mu.acquire() — vtable slot 0. *)    
      225 +    EVAL MSIRBuilder.EmitMethodCall("", mu, 0L, MSIR.TVoid(), 
      226 +                                     ARRAY OF MSIR.Value{});  
      227 +    IF NOT MSIRBuilder.InProc() THEN RETURN END;              
      228 +                                                              
      229 +    (* TRY body FINALLY mu.release() END — mirrors TryFinStmt.
          +CompileMSIR. *)                                               
      230 +    lpType := MSIR.TLandingPad();                             
      231 +    zero   := MSIR.ConstInt(MSIR.TI1(), 0L);                  
      232 +    one    := MSIR.ConstInt(MSIR.TI1(), 1L);                  
      233 +                                                              
      234 +    lpSlot  := MSIR.BuildAlloca(MSIRBuilder.CurrentBlock(), ""
          +, lpType);                                                    
      235 +    excFlag := MSIR.BuildAlloca(MSIRBuilder.CurrentBlock(), ""
          +, MSIR.TI1());                                                
      236 +    MSIR.BuildStore(MSIRBuilder.CurrentBlock(), zero, excFlag)
          +;                                                             
      237 +                                                              
      238 +    lpad      := MSIRBuilder.NewBlock("lock.lpad");           
      239 +    finBody   := MSIRBuilder.NewBlock("lock.fin");            
      240 +    resumeBlk := MSIRBuilder.NewBlock("lock.resume");         
      241 +    merge     := MSIRBuilder.NewBlock("lock.done");           
      242 +                                                              
      243 +    (* Body inside TRY (lpad = cleanup unwind target). *)     
      244 +    MSIRBuilder.PushTryContext(lpad);                         
      245 +    Stmt.CompileMSIR(p.body);                                 
      246 +    MSIRBuilder.PopTryContext();                              
      247 +                                                              
      248 +    IF NOT MSIRBuilder.CurrentBlockTerminated() THEN          
      249 +      MSIR.BuildBr(MSIRBuilder.CurrentBlock(), finBody, ARRAY 
          +OF MSIR.Value{});                                             
      250 +    END;                                                      
      251 +                                                              
      252 +    (* Landing pad: save lp value, set exceptional flag. *)   
      253 +    lpVal := MSIR.BuildLandingPad(lpad, "", isCleanup := TRUE)
          +;                                                             
      254 +    MSIR.BuildStore(lpad, lpVal, lpSlot);                     
      255 +    MSIR.BuildStore(lpad, one, excFlag);                      
      256 +    MSIR.BuildBr(lpad, finBody, ARRAY OF MSIR.Value{});       
      257 +                                                              
      258 +    (* Finally body: release the mutex — vtable slot 1.       
      259 +       Release is called outside our own lpad (already popped)
          +, so if it throws                                             
      260 +       the exception propagates to any enclosing TRY rather th
          +an looping back. *)                                           
      261 +    MSIRBuilder.SetCurrentBlock(finBody);                     
      262 +    EVAL MSIRBuilder.EmitMethodCall("", mu, 1L, MSIR.TVoid(), 
      263 +                                     ARRAY OF MSIR.Value{});  
      264 +    IF NOT MSIRBuilder.InProc() THEN RETURN END;              
      265 +                                                              
      266 +    IF NOT MSIRBuilder.CurrentBlockTerminated() THEN          
      267 +      excFlagV := MSIR.BuildLoad(MSIRBuilder.CurrentBlock(), "
          +",                                                            
      268 +                                 MSIR.TI1(), excFlag);        
      269 +      MSIR.BuildCondBr(MSIRBuilder.CurrentBlock(), excFlagV,  
      270 +                       resumeBlk, ARRAY OF MSIR.Value{},      
      271 +                       merge,     ARRAY OF MSIR.Value{});     
      272 +    END;                                                      
      273 +                                                              
      274 +    (* Resume: reload saved landing pad and resume unwinding. 
          +*)                                                            
      275 +    MSIRBuilder.SetCurrentBlock(resumeBlk);                   
      276 +    lpLoaded := MSIR.BuildLoad(resumeBlk, "", lpType, lpSlot);
      277 +    MSIR.BuildResume(resumeBlk, lpLoaded);                    
      278 +                                                              
      279 +    MSIRBuilder.SetCurrentBlock(merge);                       
      280 +  END CompileMSIR;                                            
      281 +                                                              
      282  BEGIN
      283  END LockStmt.

⏺ 67/67. Let me verify the generated IR for LockedIncr:

⏺ The structure is exactly right:
  1. Load and GC-barrier the gLock global → %t1
  2. Load vtable → %t2; load acquire fn ptr at slot 0 → %t3; call void %t3(ptr 
  %t1) — acquire
  3. Body: load gCounter, add 1, store — all as plain non-invoke calls (no outer
   TRY context)
  4. Cleanup landingpad with personality ptr @__gxx_personality_v0 (the
  LandingPad fix)
  5. lock.fin: vtable dispatch for release (slot 1), then check flag → resume or
   done

  Commit:

⏺ Done. 6757f16178 — 67 tests pass.

  What was implemented:

  LOCK mu DO body END lowers to:
  1. Acquire — mu.acquire() via vtable dispatch (slot 0 = MUTEX_acquire), before
   the try context
  2. TRY/FINALLY — body with cleanup landingpad; finally block calls
  mu.release() (slot 1 = MUTEX_release)
  3. Resume or continue — after release, check the exception flag and either
  resume the landingpad or fall through

  Bug fixed as a side effect: HasInvoke was only checking for
  invoke/InvokeIndirect to decide whether to emit personality. A LOCK body with
  no calls has a landingpad/resume but no invoke, which was silently omitting
  the required personality ptr @__gxx_personality_v0. Fixed by also detecting
  LandingPad and Resume ops.

  What's next: RAISE + exception value binding (completes EH), or runtime
  linking (type_cells, imports chain — needed for NEW to work).

⏺ Yes — and the answer to your broader question is: we should have been doing 
  this from the start. The pattern going forward is:

  Constant: GC gray/dirty bit offsets
  Source: M3RT.RH_gray_offset, M3RT.RH_dirty_offset
  Location: now used in MSIRToLLVM
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Constant: Object header size
  Source: Target.Address.bytes
  Location: now used in MSIRToLLVM, MSIRBuilder
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Constant: MUTEX vtable slots
  Source: M3RT.MUTEX_acquire / Target.Address.bytes
  Location: now used in LockStmt
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Constant: EH activation record offsets
  Source: M3RT.EA_exception = 0 (commented)
  Location: TryStmt
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Constant: RT0.GC_both = 3
  Source: literal with comment
  Location: msir doesn't see m3core
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Constant: ModuleInfo field count/layout
  Source: M3RT.MI_* offsets
  Location: still hardcoded struct, could use M3RT

  The remaining place to address is the ModuleInfo struct in EmitModuleBinder —
  it's hand-declared as 13 fields matching M3RT.MI_*. That's a natural candidate
   for a follow-up, but since the field offsets are checked at link time
  (RTLinker reads the struct by offset), correctness is verified automatically.

⏺ Done. Two commits landed from this cleanup:

  f14cf75c19 — M3RT/Target constants throughout: GC gray/dirty masks from
  M3RT.RH_gray_offset/RH_dirty_offset via Word.Shift, header size from
  Target.Address.bytes, MUTEX vtable slots from M3RT.MUTEX_acquire/MUTEX_release
   / Target.Address.bytes, vtable stride in MSIRBuilder from
  Target.Address.bytes, EA_exception offset noted in TryStmt. Added
  import("m3middle") to msir so MSIRToLLVM can reference these.

  34c277415f — ModuleInfo struct derived from M3RT: EmitModuleBinder now loops
  over M3RT.MI_SIZE / (cs × ap) fields and identifies each by comparing its byte
   offset against M3RT.MI_* / cs. Fixed a latent bug: M3RT offsets are in bits,
  not bytes — the previous code divided by Target.Address.bytes = 8 but should
  divide by Target.Address.size = 64, giving 104 fields instead of the correct
  13. Each generated field is annotated with its M3RT name.

  The pattern going forward: every CM3 runtime layout constant that appears in
  the LLVM emitter should trace back to an M3RT or Target expression — not a
  magic number.

✻ Churned for 5m 27s

❯ How about looking up RunTyme hooks?                                           

⏺ Good question. Currently any time we emit a RunTyme hook call in the MSIR path
   we either hardcode "RTHooks__Foo" as a literal string in MSIRToLLVM.m3 (the
  GC barriers, ScanTypecase) or we'll hardcode them in future CompileMSIR
  methods for RAISE, ResumeRaiseEx, NEW etc. The CG backend always looks them up
   via RunTyme.LookUpProc(RunTyme.Hook.ScanTypecase) etc.

  The right fix: add MSIRBuilder.HookProc(h) that looks up any hook through
  RunTyme and returns an MSIR.Proc stub. Every CompileMSIR method that calls
  into the runtime can then use it instead of hardcoding. The inline text in
  MSIRToLLVM.m3 for the GC barrier fast-paths can't use this (different

  The answer to your question: yes, we should use RunTyme hooks, and now there's
   a clean way to do it in any CompileMSIR method via
  MSIRBuilder.HookProc(RunTyme.Hook.X). The two places we can't use it are the
  GC barrier fast-path inline code in MSIRToLLVM.m3 (different package) and
  Op.Typecase/vtable dispatch (which don't call named hooks — they use
  structured MSIR ops). Everything else that calls RTHooks__* in future features
   should go through HookProc.

❯ I'm concerned about that last statement because w now have a dependence in

⏺ Yes, the concern is valid. MSIRToLLVM.m3 hardcodes
  "RTHooks__CheckLoadTracedRef", "RTHooks__CheckStoreTraced", and
  "RTHooks__ScanTypecase" as raw strings. These should flow from RunTyme just
  like every other hook.

  The clean solution: store hook proc references in the MSIR.Module — set from
  MSIREmit.m3 (which has RunTyme access) via HookProc, and read by MSIRToLLVM.m3
   via LLSymbol. Fallback to the hardcoded names if not set, preserving backward
   compatibility.

  Searched for 1 pattern (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ The architecture is now clean:

  Before: MSIRToLLVM.m3 had three hardcoded strings
  ("RTHooks__CheckLoadTracedRef" etc.) with no connection to the actual runtime
  interface.

  After:
  - MSIR.Module carries three Proc fields set by MSIREmit.EndUnit via
  RunTyme.LookUpProc — the same lookup path the CG backend uses
  - MSIRToLLVM.LLHookName(proc, fallback) uses LLSymbol(proc) when the proc is
  set, falling back to the hardcoded string only when NIL (e.g., standalone
  testing without the m3front/RunTyme stack)
  - Why EndUnit not BeginUnit: RunTyme.LookUpProc has <*ASSERT hooks # NIL*> —
  RTHooks is only available after the unit's imports are processed, which
  completes during body compilation, not at BeginUnit time

  The msir package itself still carries no hardwired knowledge of the runtime
  module name — all that comes through the MSIR.Module fields set by m3front.

❯ What next?                                                                    

⏺ Updated picture after this session's cleanup:

  ✅ Done: Arithmetic, control flow, arrays, records
  Remaining: RAISE — HookProc ready, needs exception descriptor static
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ✅ Done: EH: TRY/EXCEPT, TRY/FINALLY
  Remaining: Exception value binding (EXCEPT E(v) =>)
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ✅ Done: GC barriers (read + write)
  Remaining: type_cells — exception + object descriptors for NEW/RAISE
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ✅ Done: RTLinker binder + ModuleInfo (M3RT-derived)
  Remaining: NEW — needs type_cells
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ✅ Done: TYPECASE, method dispatch, field access
  Remaining: Dereference (p^) — likely a quick win
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ✅ Done: LOCK
  Remaining: Nested procs — hardest
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ✅ Done: Hook names from RunTyme (just done)
  Remaining:

  ---
  Three options:

  RAISE + exception value binding — the natural next step given all the EH
  infrastructure. With HookProc ready, RAISE needs: a static ExceptionDescriptor
   global per EXCEPTION declaration ({ uid, name_ptr }) and a stack-allocated
  RaiseActivation struct filled with the descriptor pointer and arg (NIL for
  no-arg), then HookProc(Raise) called with its address. Exception value binding
   (EXCEPT E(v) =>) then needs ~20 more lines. Together these complete the EH
  story end-to-end.

  Dereference (p^) — DerefExpr.CompileMSIR probably doesn't exist yet and it's
  needed for any code that uses REF T values explicitly (loading through a
  pointer). Likely a 10-line fix that unblocks many abandoned procs.

  type_cells + NEW — generates the object/exception type descriptors into
  type_cells in the ModuleInfo, enabling both NEW(T) (heap allocation) and
  correct RAISE/catch matching at runtime. Medium effort but unlocks the whole
  heap-object story.

  ---
  My recommendation: Check DerefExpr first (it's a 10-minute fix and may unblock
   a lot), then RAISE + exception value binding to close EH. type_cells + NEW is
   a larger chunk that stands alone as a session.

  Want to look at DerefExpr?

  fine. The smoke module now compiles completely (56 proc definitions, 0
  abandonments, 0 unhandled ops).

  The real picture at this point:

  The smoke test is fully covered. Abandonment now happens only in modules that
  use features we haven't implemented yet:
  - RAISE — any proc that raises an exception
  - Exception value binding (EXCEPT E(v) =>)
  - NEW — any proc that allocates heap objects
  - Nested procedures — up-level variable access
  - String operations (TEXT concat, IO.Put from module body)

  These require either looking at a richer test module or moving on to the next
  feature. The most impactful next target remains RAISE + exception descriptors,
   which would complete the exception-handling story. Ready to start on that?

      101 +PROCEDURE CompileMSIR (p: P) =                                
      102 +  (* RAISE E  or  RAISE E(arg)                                
      103 +     RTHooks__Raise(ex, arg, module, line):                   
      104 +       ex     = direct pointer to ExceptionDesc { uid, name_pt
          +r, implicit }                                                 
      105 +       arg    = argument value cast to ptr (NIL for no-arg exc
          +eptions)                                                      
      106 +       module = NIL (diagnostics only; skip for now)          
      107 +       line   = 0  (diagnostics only; skip for now)           
      108 +     RTHooks__Raise never returns — emit unreachable after the
          + call. *)                                                     
      109 +  VAR                                                         
      110 +    raiseProc : MSIR.Proc;                                    
      111 +    descVal   : MSIR.Value;                                   
      112 +    argVal    : MSIR.Value;                                   
      113 +    ptrT      := MSIR.TPtr(MSIR.TVoid());                     
      114 +    args      : REF ARRAY OF MSIR.Value;                      
      115 +  BEGIN                                                       
      116 +    IF NOT MSIRBuilder.InProc() THEN RETURN END;              
      117 +    IF p.except = NIL THEN                                    
      118 +      MSIRBuilder.Abandon("raise: exception not resolved");   
      119 +      RETURN;                                                 
      120 +    END;                                                      
      121 +                                                              
      122 +    raiseProc := MSIRBuilder.HookProc(RunTyme.Hook.RaiseEx);  
      123 +    IF raiseProc = NIL THEN                                   
      124 +      MSIRBuilder.Abandon("raise: RTHooks__Raise not available
          +");                                                           
      125 +      RETURN;                                                 
      126 +    END;                                                      
      127 +                                                              
      128 +    (* Exception descriptor pointer. *)                       
      129 +    descVal := MSIRBuilder.ExcDescValue(p.except);            
      130 +    IF descVal = NIL THEN                                     
      131 +      MSIRBuilder.Abandon("raise: cannot build exception descr
          +iptor");                                                      
      132 +      RETURN;                                                 
      133 +    END;                                                      
      134 +                                                              
      135 +    (* Argument: compile if present, else NIL. *)             
      136 +    IF p.arg # NIL THEN                                       
      137 +      argVal := Expr.CompileMSIR(p.arg);                      
      138 +      IF argVal = NIL THEN RETURN END;                        
      139 +      (* Cast to ptr (ADDRESS) for RTHooks__Raise parameter. *
          +)                                                             
      140 +      argVal := MSIR.BuildConvert(MSIRBuilder.CurrentBlock(), 
          +"", argVal, ptrT);                                            
      141 +    ELSE                                                      
      142 +      argVal := MSIR.ConstNil(ptrT);                          
      143 +    END;                                                      
      144 +                                                              
      145 +    args := NEW(REF ARRAY OF MSIR.Value, 4);                  
      146 +    args[0] := descVal;                                       
      147 +    args[1] := argVal;                                        
      148 +    args[2] := MSIR.ConstNil(ptrT);    (* module: NIL *)      
      149 +    args[3] := MSIR.ConstInt(MSIR.TI(64), 0L);  (* line: 0 *) 
      150 +                                                              
      151 +    EVAL MSIRBuilder.EmitCall("", raiseProc, args^);

  Let me check:

  Looking at LLOpVal:
  | MSIR.ValueKind.GlobalRef =>
      Wr.PutText(wr, "@");

  bypass LLGlobalSym in LLOpVal.

⏺ Found it. The landingpad's extractvalue ... 0 gives the exception header (a
  _Unwind_Exception), not the thrown _M3Exc object. Clang calls
  @__cxa_begin_catch(header) -> exception_object before accessing the thrown
  value. Our TryStmt.CompileMSIR skips that call, so it reads from the header
  (which starts with the ABI magic "CLNGC++") instead of from _M3Exc.act.

  The fix: add __cxa_begin_catch around catch landing pads and __cxa_end_catch
  after each handler and before resume:

⏺ Update(~/git/cm3/m3-sys/msir/src/MSIRToLLVM.m3)
  ⎿  Added 5 lines
      1155      IF needsEH THEN
      1156        Wr.PutText(wr, "@_ZTI6_M3Exc = external constant ptr\n"
            );
      1157        Wr.PutText(wr, "declare i32 @__gxx_personality_v0(...)\
            n");
      1158 +      (* Itanium C++ ABI helpers required by catch landingpad
           +s.                                                           
      1159 +         __cxa_begin_catch converts the exception-header ptr 
           +(from extractvalue)                                          
      1160 +         to the actual thrown exception object ptr.  __cxa_en
           +d_catch releases it. *)                                      
      1161 +      Wr.PutText(wr, "declare ptr  @__cxa_begin_catch(ptr)\n"
           +);

⏺ 68/68 pass. Here's what was built:

  RAISE E end-to-end — the first full exception-raise path through the MSIR →
  LLVM pipeline:

  1. Exception descriptor static global — @Main__TestExcept_excptr = internal 
  global { i64, ptr, i64 } with uid = M3FP(name), created lazily and emitted
  before the module binder.
  2. RaiseStmt.CompileMSIR — calls RTHooks__Raise(desc, null, null, 0) via
  HookProc(RaiseEx) and emits unreachable after.
  3. __cxa_begin_catch ABI fix — the big discovery: the Itanium C++ ABI
  landingpad's field 0 is the exception header (_Unwind_Exception*, starting
  with "CLNGC++" = 0x434c4e47432b2b00), not the thrown _M3Exc object.
  __cxa_begin_catch(header) returns the actual object pointer. Without this
  call, the UID load chain read the header's ABI magic bytes as a pointer and
  crashed. Now: header → __cxa_begin_catch → _M3Exc* → .act → .exception → .uid.
  4. __cxa_end_catch on no-match and fall-through — correctly balances the
  begin_catch. Known gap: ret inside a handler body skips __cxa_end_catch
  (exception object leaks; no crash).
  5. raise_stub.cpp — provides a proper C++ struct _M3Exc throw so the
  personality function can match the LLVM IR's catch ptr @_ZTI6_M3Exc. Removing
  the old null-array _ZTI6_M3Exc stub was key — the C++ compiler auto-generates
  valid typeinfo when it compiles the throw.

✻ Sautéed for 1m 40s

❯ DId you say there are memory leaks in EH? Should we not use heap to avoid?    

⏺ Good catch to question this. Let me clarify exactly what's happening, because
  "memory leak" was imprecise.

  What the leak actually is:

  The C++ exception object is heap-allocated by __cxa_allocate_exception inside
  RTStack__ThrowM3Exc — that's the C++ runtime's own internal allocation, not
  the M3 GC heap. __cxa_begin_catch claims ownership (increments a refcount),
  and __cxa_end_catch releases it. Without the matching __cxa_end_catch in
  matched handlers that exit via ret, that C++ allocation stays live until
  thread exit. It's a C++ runtime-level leak, not M3 heap.

  A cleaner fix — use __cxa_get_exception_ptr for peeking:

  __cxa_get_exception_ptr gives you the thrown object pointer WITHOUT acquiring
  ownership (no __cxa_end_catch needed). We only need
  __cxa_begin_catch/__cxa_end_catch around the actual handler body. That
  eliminates the pairing problem entirely:

  ; Current (problematic):
    %exc_obj = call ptr @__cxa_begin_catch(ptr %exc_header)  ; acquires 
  ownership
    ; ... load UID, compare ...
    ; if match: handler body → __cxa_end_catch() (MISSING for ret paths)
    ; if no match: __cxa_end_catch() + resume

  ; Better:
    %exc_obj = call ptr @__cxa_get_exception_ptr(ptr %exc_header)  ; peek, no 
  ownership
    ; ... load UID, compare ...
    ; if match: __cxa_begin_catch + handler body + __cxa_end_catch (always 
  paired)
    ; if no match: resume (no cleanup needed — never acquired ownership)

  This is how Clang actually generates it in some cases. It's cleaner because:
  1. No ownership until we commit to a specific handler
  2. __cxa_begin_catch/__cxa_end_catch are strictly paired within the handler
  body — no need to thread context through ReturnStmt
  3. resume in the no-match path needs no cleanup

  Shall I implement this now? It's a targeted change to TryStmt.CompileMSIR —
  replace CxaBeginCatch at the top with __cxa_get_exception_ptr, then wrap
  CxaBeginCatch/CxaEndCatch tightly around each handler body.

✻ Worked for 36s

❯ Yes                                                                           

⏺ The fix: use __cxa_get_exception_ptr (peek, no ownership) for the UID
  comparison, and only call __cxa_begin_catch/__cxa_end_catch bracketing the
  actual handler body. Then add a "catch context" stack to MSIRBuilder so
  ReturnStmt.CompileMSIR automatically emits __cxa_end_catch before any ret
  inside a handler.

⏺ The structure is now correct and leak-free:

  - lpad.1: __cxa_get_exception_ptr — peek at exception object, no ownership
  acquired

  ⎿  [msir 98f7c83623] msir: fix EH catch-handler ownership — use __cxa_get
     _exception_ptr for peeking
      5 files changed, 87 insertions(+), 37 deletions(-)

⏺ The leak is gone. The structure is now correct for the full ownership
  lifecycle:

  - Peeking (UID comparison): __cxa_get_exception_ptr — read-only access, no
  ownership, no cleanup needed on any path
  - Handling (matched handler): __cxa_begin_catch acquires ownership at the top
  of the handler; __cxa_end_catch is emitted before every exit — fall-through
  via explicit call, ret via ReturnStmt.CompileMSIR checking
  CurrentCatchEndProc()
  - Resuming (no match): plain resume — ownership was never acquired, so no
  release needed

  The key insight: separate "looking at the exception to decide if you want it"
  (get_exception_ptr) from "claiming it" (begin_catch). This eliminates the
  pairing problem entirely.

  ⎿  [msir 4d4da36a0e] docs: remove stale __cxa_end_catch leak note — fixed
      in previous commit

⏺ Yes, fully fixed. The previous commit (98f7c83623) resolved it completely:

  - Before: __cxa_begin_catch at the top of the landing pad acquired ownership
  before knowing which handler would run. ret paths missed __cxa_end_catch.
  - After: __cxa_get_exception_ptr peeks without acquiring ownership. Ownership
  is only acquired inside the matched handler body with __cxa_begin_catch, and
  __cxa_end_catch is emitted on every exit path — fall-through via explicit
  call, ret via ReturnStmt.CompileMSIR checking CurrentCatchEndProc(). The
  no-match resume path never acquired ownership and needs no cleanup.

  The docs are updated accordingly.

  ---
  Three options:

  Exception value binding (C) — the last piece of the EH story. EXCEPT E(v) =>
  with a bound variable currently falls back to body-only. The fix: in the
  handler body, load the arg field of the RaiseActivation at EA_arg = 8 bytes
  offset and store it into the bound variable's alloca. ~30 lines in
  TryStmt.CompileMSIR, but needs ExcDescValue to also record the arg type so we
  know what to store.

  Runtime linking / type_cells (A) — the most foundational remaining item.
  Without type_cells, NEW(T) can't allocate typed heap objects,
  Compiler.ThisException() doesn't work, and the standalone program path is
  incomplete. Medium effort; would enable NEW in one go.

  NEW — heap allocation — unblocked once type_cells exists.
  RTHooks__AllocateTracedObj(typeDescr, size) or AllocateTracedRef. With NEW,
  real OO tests become possible.

  ---
  My recommendation: exception value binding — it's a small, self-contained
  30-line addition that completes the EH story we've been building all session.
  Then runtime linking + NEW as the next larger chunk.

  What would you like to do?

❯ Do we need to worry about conversions ptr to add or ref to addr?              

⏺ Good question. Let me check what conversions can arise in practice and test
  the current bitcast fallback against LLVM:

⏺ Bash(# Test whether LLVM 22 accepts bitcast ptr to ptr
      echo '%r = bitcast ptr %x to ptr' | $(brew --prefix llvm)/bin/llvm-as - -o
       /dev/null 2>&1 || true…)
  ⎿  /opt/homebrew/opt/llvm/bin/llvm-as: <stdin>:1:6: error: expected 'type
     ' after name
     %r = bitcast ptr %x to ptr
          ^

⏺ Bash(cat > /tmp/ptrtest.ll << 'EOF'
      define ptr @test(ptr %x) {…)
  ⎿  (No output)

  Searched for 1 pattern (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Short answer: no additional work needed.

  bitcast ptr %x to ptr is accepted by LLVM 22 (verified above), and it doesn't
  currently appear in any generated IR — no M3 code in the smoke test needs
  ptr↔addr conversions yet.

  The mapping is:
  - ADDRESS, UNTRACED REF T → ptr (LLVM opaque pointer)
  - REF T, REFANY → ptr (same LLVM type — no distinction)
  - gc_ref, gc_slot → also ptr at the LLVM level

  Because all pointer flavors map to the same LLVM ptr type in opaque-pointer
  mode (LLVM 15+), any ptr → ptr bitcast is a no-op that LLVM accepts and
  immediately eliminates. The two cases we just fixed were the genuinely
  different ones: i64 → ptr (inttoptr) and ptr → i64 (ptrtoint), which LLVM
  cannot represent as bitcast.

  The only future concern is GC semantic correctness — if LOOPHOLE(ref, ADDRESS)
   converts a traced REF T to an untraced ADDRESS, the GC should still scan the
  original variable. But that's a property of the variable declaration (it stays
   in the GC root map as gc_ref/gc_slot), not of the LOOPHOLE expression result.
   The LLVM IR correctly generates bitcast ptr to ptr and the GC handles roots
  through the variable's alloca, not through transient expression values.

✻ Crunched for 1m 15s

⏺ The EH story is now fully complete. Updated state:

  ✅ Done: Arithmetic, control flow, arrays, records
  Remaining: A. Runtime linking — imports chain, type_cells, GC maps
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ✅ Done: EH: TRY/EXCEPT, TRY/FINALLY
  Remaining: NEW — needs type_cells
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ✅ Done: RAISE (exc descriptors, RTHooks__Raise)
  Remaining: G. Nested procedures — up-level access, hardest
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ✅ Done: Exception value binding (EXCEPT E(v) =>)
  Remaining:
  ────────────────────────────────────────

  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ✅ Done: GC barriers, RTLinker binder, TYPECASE
  Remaining:
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ✅ Done: Method dispatch, heap field access, LOCK
  Remaining:

  ---
  Three remaining directions:

  Runtime linking (A) — the foundational pillar. Emit type_cells (exception +
  object type descriptors), the imports chain (so RTLinker can transitively
  initialize dependencies), and var_map/gc_map (so the GC scans module globals).
   Once type_cells exists, NEW(T) works and the standalone program path is
  complete. Medium effort — probably 2-3 sessions.

  NEW — directly blocked on type_cells. RTHooks__AllocateTracedObj(typeDescr, 
  dataSize) and RTHooks__AllocateTracedRef are straightforward calls once
  descriptors are available. The Op.New insn is already in MSIR.

  Nested procedures (G) — up-level variable access via static links. This
  requires extending MSIRBuilder with a display/chain mechanism. The hardest
  remaining item and the least commonly needed (most M3 code doesn't use nested
  procs heavily).

  ---
  My recommendation: runtime linking. It's the most impactful unlock — once
  type_cells is there, both NEW and correct exception registration work, the
  standalone binary runs cleanly end-to-end, and the smoke test no longer needs
  C stubs for IO.Put/Fmt.Int. The imports chain also means transitive module
  initialization works, which is foundational for any real application.

  Want to start on that, or tackle NEW independently first as a warm-up?

✻ Worked for 32s
❯ Do A                                                                          

⏺ Let me study what the import chain needs and how Module.m3 builds it:

  Searched for 1 pattern, read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Good. The core of runtime linking is the imports chain — a linked list of
  ImportInfo { import_ptr=null, binder_fn, next } records in the ModuleInfo.
  BuildImportLink in Module.m3 builds the CG version; I'll hook into it to also
  register binders with MSIR. Then EmitModuleBinder emits the chain.
